# Follow-up register

This is the one register of every follow-up the Orbit parity program has raised: the Known gaps in
[untrusted-repositories.md](untrusted-repositories.md), the follow-ups of every pull request, and the open items of each
milestone's verification rounds. It covers milestones M1 and M2, the M3 task pull requests, M3 verification rounds 1
to 9, the M4 task pull requests, M4 verification rounds 1 to 11, and the minor findings of M4 verification rounds 12 to 17 (their re-check regressions marked MINOR; those rounds raised no other follow-up). The class numbers are the classes of
[untrusted-repositories.md](untrusted-repositories.md).

## How a row moves

- Every follow-up has one row with a stable id, `FU-<milestone>-<nnn>`, where the milestone is the one it was first
  raised in. The same defect found again, or worded differently, is one row that lists every source and keeps the status
  and carrier of the latest check; the other ids are in "Duplicates", each with the row it was merged into.
- A row is **open** (it names a carrier: a plan task id, `trust-first`, `sweep` for work no task owns, or `owner`), **owner**
  (it waits for a decision, one line below), **fixed**, or a **duplicate**.
- A **fixed** row names the commit and the test that pins it. A row with no commit stays open, and so does a code fix that
  no test pins ("Fix not pinned by a test", carrier `sweep`). A fix to a document, a comment, a build file or the tests
  themselves says "none needed" or "the fix is the test" and why. When a later round finds a fix reverted, the row is open
  again and says so (FU rows with "reopened" in their source).
- Each milestone's verification adds its rows and updates the statuses it checked. A task that carries a row closes it in
  its own pull request: it moves the row to "Fixed" with the commit and the test.
- Severity is the severity the checking round gave it. "Open majors" lists every open row of severity major, whatever
  its carrier, and those rows are not repeated in the milestone tables. "For the owner" holds the rows whose status is
  owner (they wait for a decision before anyone can build); an open row whose carrier is `owner` has no plan task and
  sits in the last open table.
- A carrier is a plan task id. Three tasks (`clients-ux-8`, `clients-ux-26`, `agents-extensibility-34`) appear in the plan
  only by their short ids (CU8, CU26, AE34). `trust-first` is the task that the owner placed between M4 and M5 (plan,
  "Owner decisions during M4 verification": nothing from a project folder loads or runs until the folder is trusted); a
  row whose defect is something a folder supplies before trust carries it and closes with it. A milestone closes with its
  integration pull request, so after M4 no open row names an M4 task as its carrier.
- The evidence column of an open row says where the defect is (file and line) as the triage found it. The M4 rows (the
  follow-ups of the M4 task pull requests and of M4 verification rounds 1 to 11, the 69 older rows that M4 work added,
  closed or reopened, and every fixed row) were re-checked against the tree at `7eb4a0ac` (the head of `origin/m4-integration`
  when this update was written): a fixed row's commit is an ancestor of that head, and its test name is in the tree where
  the row gives one. For an open row the triage read the evidence at that head and a script checked that each cited file
  exists and each cited line is inside it; a line number is not checked against the text it points at, and a review
  found two stale ones that were then corrected (FU-M4-102, FU-M4-177). The follow-ups of M3 verification rounds 7
  to 9 were re-checked against `bcfcfd7` (the head of `m3-integration` then). Five rows that an earlier round called
  fixed were reopened because the anti-loop reverts of rounds 8 and 9 (`e94ae2f`, `4c88f0b`) removed their code and
  tests: three are open rows (FU-M3-242, FU-M3-288, FU-M3-290) and two became duplicates of them. Line numbers into
  [untrusted-repositories.md](untrusted-repositories.md) and [known-failures.md](known-failures.md) were adjusted for the
  lines this pull request adds to them; a heading cited by name does not move.

Counts at the end of this file.

## For the owner

No row waits for a decision. The owner-row decisions of M4 verification round 2 (2026-10-19) moved every other row that was here to its carrier's table or to "Fixed", and FU-M4-316 was closed by the owner's rule of 2026-10-20 (follow the reference).

## Open, by carrier milestone

Open rows of severity minor, grouped by the plan milestone of the carrier task. `trust-first` is the task that the owner placed between M4 and M5 (it stops anything in a project folder from loading or running before the folder is trusted); it closed its nine rows, which are in "Fixed", so it has no table. Its part 2 closed its rows, which are in "Fixed", and part 3, `trust-first-cleanup`, carries no row. "Sweep" is work no task owns. M4 has no table: M4 closes with this update, and no open row names an M4 task as its carrier.

| FU-M4-r1-prompt-keys-no-fingerprint | The `settings` kind is not part of the Fingerprint | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): the `settings` kind (output_style, language, attribution, isolation and the like) is not added to `Fingerprint` (packages/core/src/config/fingerprint.ts:27-38 lists the digested kinds). The reference applies later edits to a trusted project's settings without asking again, and the values run nothing. Found again by prompts-context-21 task review (T136) | none needed |
| FU-M1-013 | A child given a relative PATH entry still searches it | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides; accepted in writing by the owner's rules of 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-18, FU-M1-078): Accept the gap. The reference sets the no-current-folder lookup variable for its own program lookups and hands children the person's PATH as it is. Only the person's own PATH or config can name a relative entry, because an untrusted project's env is withheld. This also lets FU-M4-001 find the program Orbit starts with which. | none needed |
| FU-M1-026 | Mapped network drives and shell-built words escape containment | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides; accepted in writing by the owner's rules of 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-18, FU-M1-078): Accept the gap. The reference treats a drive letter as an ordinary path (its share checks look only for `\\` and `//` spellings), and only asks about shell input it cannot analyse. Shell-built words stay with shell-safety-12. | none needed |
| FU-M1-050 | A Unicode-host URL rule must be written in punycode | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides; accepted in writing by the owner's rules of 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-18, FU-M1-078): Accept the gap. The reference compares its rule with the URL parser's ASCII host exactly, so a rule written with Unicode never matches there either. The ASCII form is documented at permission/web.ts:16. | none needed |
| FU-M1-058 | Workspace-trust design choices taken at merge without a written record | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): No change. All three choices match the reference: no project setting applies before trust (AGENTS.md class 8 now states the agent rule), the whole session waits for trust whoever configured a tool, and a grant never covers the folders above it. The owner's 2026-10-14 rule is the written basis. | none needed |
| FU-M1-060 | Headless runs go ahead while config above a trusted project is held back | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): No change. The reference never refuses a headless run over trust. Orbit runs the trusted project with the config above it held back, and already prints the warning (cli/src/util/trust.ts:103). | none needed |
| FU-M1-080 | releaseChildClaims clears claims without a terminal event | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides; accepted in writing by the owner's rules of 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-18, FU-M1-078): Accept the missing terminal event. The reference keeps no durable record of a subagent that a crash cut off, and FU-M1-079's option (b) closes the turn-boundary effect. | none needed |
| FU-M1-088 | Trait predicates have no time limit and are not re-read after execute.before | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides; accepted in writing by the owner's rules of 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-18, FU-M1-078): Accept the gap. The reference decides whether a call may run alongside others once, from the model's parsed input when the call is queued. That check is synchronous, runs before hooks, has no time limit, counts a throw as not safe, and is not repeated after a hook changes the input. | none needed |
| FU-M1-089 | conservativeFor wording awaits owner confirmation | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Keep conservativeFor and its wording. When a trait check fails, the reference falls back on that trait and keeps everything the tool declares about itself. Orbit does the same and is stricter on the traits computed from the input. | none needed |
| FU-M1-091 | response.completed with an unknown reason releases a cut-off call | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. On a completed response the reference's Responses adapter ignores any reason and reports a tool-use stop whenever it saw a call, so Orbit's tool-calls mapping matches. A conforming server closes each call's arguments before completion. | none needed |
| FU-M1-092 | A stop sequence that cuts a tool call releases the call | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference sends stop sequences only on side queries that carry no tools. Orbit's built-ins set none, so only a trusted plugin's hook can set stop, and an untrusted folder cannot load such a plugin. | none needed |
| FU-M1-096 | VS Code does not re-read trust after a failed first read | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): No change needed. The reference checks trust at the point of use. In VS Code, send re-reads trust in gate() before any work, and usable() reads again while nothing has answered, so a failed first read only delays the banner. | none needed |
| FU-M1-100 | A rerouting hook resets any route-level chunkTimeout to 300 s | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference has one idle watchdog value, set only by the person's environment, that no request path changes, and it has no rerouting hook. In Orbit only the first-party route sets a route default, so a rerouted request behaves as it did before PR #33. | none needed |
| FU-M2-035 | The session.retry hook cannot see the fallback switch | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference has no hook event for retries or for the fallback switch; the switch is shown to the person as a notice, as in Orbit. | none needed |
| FU-M2-038 | The steered flag never resets when a steer is withdrawn | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference's submit interrupt is a one-way abort of the turn, and taking the queued message back into the input does not undo it. | none needed |
| FU-M2-039 | A steer pending when a new attempt starts does not cancel that attempt's calls | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference decides cancellation at submit time from the tools running then, so calls that start later are not cancelled. | none needed |
| FU-M2-116 | A correction halts the queue only after the race and execute.after hooks | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. In the reference a rejection with feedback does not abort, and siblings keep running. Orbit's halt is stricter, and its late window needs a person's own slow plugin. | none needed |
| FU-M2-125 | Service config migration writes through a dangling link on Windows | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference writes its own config through links on purpose. The folder involved is the person's own state folder, which no repository can name. | none needed |
| FU-M3-017 | Overlay run's session list, tabs and /resume can open a session the service runs (hand leases to the service) | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference takes no lock on resume, so two processes can continue the same session; its process registry only lists them. | none needed |
| FU-M3-025 | Provider settings overlay across managed files (a later baseURL keeps an earlier apiKey) | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference deep-merges settings sources and managed drop-ins key by key, as Orbit does, and untrusted folders cannot set providers at all. | none needed |
| FU-M3-110 | Per-server LSP save: false | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference sends a save notice to every language server after every edit and has no per-server switch to turn it off. The plan appendix keeps 'add when Rust users report load'. | none needed |
| FU-M3-133 | A file the person names inside an untrusted clone is fully trusted | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference treats the settings file the person names on the command line as a trusted source, wherever it is. Naming it is the person's own choice, like --trust. | none needed |
| FU-M3-138 | Window between assertUnchanged and the write (outside writers) | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference's synchronous check-then-write section leaves the same window open to writers outside the process, and the plan appendix rejects temp-file-and-rename as still racy. | none needed |
| FU-M3-161 | Page text can steer the small model or close `<page>` early | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference gives the page and question to its small model with the same exposure: the page can steer the answer and can break out of the delimiter. The answer is still tool output, which the main model treats as data, so the promise is untouched. | none needed |
| FU-M3-176 | Unreadable managed file loses managed_only (fail-open) | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): Accept the gap. The reference drops a managed file that cannot be read or does not validate, keeps the other sources and reports the error. The plan appendix keeps fail-open. A managed file is the administrator's, not the repository's. | none needed |
| FU-M3-243 | os.tmpdir() is not always internal (reverses the plan appendix) | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): No change: Orbit already matches the reference. The reference treats only its own per-project temp folder and the session scratchpad as internal; the system temp folder is outside, and the sandbox's writable folders count only while sandboxed. The plan appendix line is superseded, and the record says so. | none needed |
| FU-M3-399 | A managed external_directory allow must cover the link's folder: one naming only the real place behind a link never lifts the built-in ask (matches the reference; fails closed) | none needed (owner 2026-10-18: reference decides): No change: Orbit already matches the reference. The reference checks deny and ask rules against the path and the real place a link leads to, but an allow rule only against the path as written. An allow naming only the real place never lifts the ask for the link's folder, and the result fails closed. The upgrading note already says the allow must cover the link's folder. | none needed |
| FU-M1-016 | VS Code code that loads vscode is untested | none needed (owner decision 2026-10-19) | none needed (no extension-host harness; a recorded manual check of the banner, status bar item, dialog, command and terminals runs before each milestone closes) |

## Duplicates

Each entry is a follow-up that is the same defect as an existing row (or, for the wildcard read check and the git output
placement, a requirement that was written against code that no longer exists). The row it points to lists the defect.

- The committed PowerShell share row runs with the no-host parser, so its word is opaque and never expanded (M3 verification round 7, follow-up 4) -> FU-M3-288
- Windows: a root-relative output or -C value after a -C on another drive or a share is resolved on the working folder's drive (M3 verification round 7, follow-up 5) -> FU-M3-416
- PowerShell (the default shell on Windows) never records git's --output/-o files, and docs/upgrading.md says the guard records them without saying 'bash only' (M3 verification round 7, follow-up 6) -> FU-M3-416
- Wildcard matching inside one folder listing ignores the 3-second deadline: one command can block the server for about 49 s (M3 verification round 7, follow-up 15) -> FU-M3-288
- An exact 'always' save of text that holds `*` or `?` is still a wildcard pattern (M3 verification round 7, follow-up 16) -> FU-M3-242
- The bash and zsh startup options a nested launch takes are not read for anything but wildcard detection; zsh's own globstar is keyed to the outer shell only (M3 verification round 7, follow-up 17) -> FU-M3-288

- A startup-file variable given as a prefix (`BASH_ENV=rc bash -c 'cat *'`, `ZDOTDIR=. zsh -c 'cat *'`) does not make the word undecided, while `env BASH_ENV=rc bash -c` and `--rcfile` do (M3 verification round 7, follow-up 21) -> FU-M3-288
- `?` and bracket classes match one UTF-16 unit, so `cat k?.txt` misses a denied `k😀.txt` (M3 verification round 7, follow-up 22) -> FU-M3-288
